\section{Small-parameter uniqueness and local continuity}
\label{sec:provenance-big-small-uniqueness}

\Cref{sec:ytd-big-small-uniqueness} follows
\cite{DangZhangZhou26UniquenessV1}, arXiv:2609.00810v1,
submitted 1 September 2026, with manuscript date 2 September.
The complete twenty-one-page extracted text, including references,
was read and every actual PDF page inspected. The controlling
anchors are Theorem~2.2 for uniform exponential integrability,
Theorem~3.2 for density stability, Theorem~3.3 for the known
small-parameter conclusion, and Theorem~4.1 and the proof of
Theorem~1.2 for logarithmic continuity. The chapter retains a
smooth compact K\"ahler manifold, positive big-class volume,
fixed geometry, probability measures and minimal singularities.
It makes no assertion in the arbitrary normal klt category.

There are two adjustments to the proof of Theorem~3.3.
On actual page~12, a supremum of exponential differences is
used in a Lipschitz estimate. Minimal singularities in a merely
big class do not ensure that this supremum is finite.
The chapter instead uses a smaller exponent $1<q<p$, a uniform
$L^q$ density bound, the exact estimate
$|\log Z_{u_0}-\log Z_{v_0}|\leq\lambda\|u_0-v_0\|_\infty$,
and a pointwise exponential comparison inside the $L^{nq}$ norm.
On actual page~11 the source describes the supremum-zero psh
slice as convex. That slice need not be convex. The chapter uses
the integral-zero slice, solves the normalized ordinary full-mass
equation, and proves continuity of the self-map from higher
integrability and Theorem~3.2 before applying Schauder.
These are explicitly identified direct formal consequences of
the cited inputs, not new priority claims or a counterexample
to the small-parameter theorem. No independent production-model
receipt is asserted.

The auxiliary density printed in the proof of Theorem~3.1 on
actual page~8 has an unpowered root difference. That proof is
outside the chapter's route: both the contraction and the
integral-normalized fixed point use Theorem~3.2. Its extrapolated
obstacle has a psh minorant because it is $V_\theta$ plus a
bounded function. Selected checks in
\cite{DDL23RelativeV2}, arXiv:2303.11584v2, cover contact
concentration in Theorem~2.7, comparison on a contact set in
Lemma~2.9, and the envelope normalization in Lemma~3.5, on
actual pages~14--17 and~23. The published survey's locators
are not substituted for those checked in this version.
The ordinary full-mass existence and minimal-singularity inputs
are Theorems~A and~B of \cite{BEGZ10BigV2}.

The source uses $\ddc_s=\rmi\partial\bar\partial$, whereas
the book uses $\ddc_b=\rmi\partial\bar\partial/(2\pi)$.
To put the book's class into the source's volume-one convention,
set $c=V^{-1/n}$, $\Theta=c\theta$ and
$U=cu/(2\pi)$. Then
\[
 \Theta+\ddc_sU=c(\theta+\ddc_bu),\qquad
 \operatorname{vol}_s(\{\Theta\})=1,\qquad
 \left\langle(\Theta+\ddc_sU)^n\right\rangle
       =\operatorname{MA}_\theta(u).
\]
The normalized density is unchanged. The source parameter is
$\lambda_s=2\pi V^{1/n}\lambda_b$, and a difference norm is
multiplied by $c/(2\pi)$. Thus constants and the sufficiently
small parameter bound are rescaled; parameter units are not
silently identified. A constant scaling of the reference
K\"ahler volume realizes the probability volume, and its distance
scaling changes only the local modulus constants.

The local power modulus is a separate selected import from
\cite[Theorem~D]{DDGHKZ14HolderPublished}, checked in the
publisher PDF on actual pages~4 and~23--25, printed pages~622
and~641--643. Its domain includes any big class, with an $L^q$
right-hand density and exponent below $2/(1+nq')$ on the
ample locus. This is not inferred from a logarithmic modulus.
The logarithmic subsection retains
$V_\theta-M_0\leq\varphi\leq V_\theta$ as an explicit
hypothesis. It does not import the auxiliary uniform-estimate
argument cited by the September source to establish this bound
from an Orlicz density alone; that argument's admissibility is
still being audited.

In the regularization argument on actual page~16 of the
September source, the analytic barrier is shifted down before
comparing it with the regularized obstacle. This makes the
positive-part $L^1$ estimate valid without changing positivity.
The local average-to-oscillation step was checked against
\cite[Lemma~4.4]{LPT21StabilityPublished}, on actual pages~23--25,
and \cite[Lemma~3.3 and condition~(3.1)]{Zeriahi20ModulusV1},
on actual pages~9--12. The former is a global bounded Hermitian
power-modulus lemma; the latter supplies the interior logarithmic
version with nested domains. Only small positive distances are
used. None of these four dependency papers was read in full
in this checkpoint. The double-logarithmic extension, unrestricted
Orlicz stability, Ricci normalization and all-parameter uniqueness
are outside the import. All problem statuses and the general
Siu, closure and quantization boundaries remain fixed.

\section{Positive parameters and the local mean-field equation}
\label{sec:provenance-meanfield-local-projective}

The two final subsections of
\Cref{sec:ytd-big-small-uniqueness} use
\cite{LuPhung25MeanFieldV1}, arXiv:2501.18281v1, submitted
30~January~2025. The complete 15-page source, including references,
was read and every actual PDF page inspected. The locators here
refer to the arXiv file, not to a later journal pagination.
The admitted material is the known projective family of
Example~3.5 and bounded zero-boundary uniqueness on hyperconvex
domains from Theorem~1.2, Lemmas~2.2--2.3 and Theorems~2.5--2.6.
No Hermitian extension or general compact stability proof from
this source is imported in this checkpoint.

The projective calculation uses the book's hyperplane-class form
$\omega_H=\ddc_b\log(1+\|z\|^2)$ of volume one.
The determinant gives the coefficient $\varepsilon$, and
$-\log\varepsilon/(n+1)$ fixes the additive constant in
\eqref{eq:ytd-meanfield-projective-equation}. The source's
unscaled Fubini--Study form uses $\ddc_s=2\ddc_b$.
Dividing that form and its currents by two yields the displayed
hyperplane-class family. The parameter $n+1$ is tied to this
class, while the solutions lie in an automorphism orbit.
It is not an obstruction to uniqueness modulo automorphisms,
a sharp threshold for arbitrary density or an endpoint
counterexample to a strictly subcritical existence statement.

The local proof keeps bounded potentials, zero boundary values,
ordinary Monge--Amp\`ere measures and mass one. Its source units
are converted by $u_s=u_b/2$, $\gamma_s=2\gamma_b$ and
$\beta_s=2\beta_b$. The criterion
$\gamma\|u\|_\infty<n$ and sufficient range are therefore
invariant under the combined conversion. The actual formula
on source page~6 in the first estimate of Theorem~2.6 writes
the denominator at $\beta/2$ instead of at the equation's
parameter. The book's identified direct calculation retains
$Z_\gamma$ and then uses $Z_\gamma\geq1$; the claimed final
estimate follows without that replacement. This is an
elementary correction of the intermediate comparison, with
no original-result or theorem-counterexample claim.

On actual page~9, the compact stability proof selects a set by

at most one half to normalize an auxiliary density. That
part of the compact proof is not imported; the preceding

route and version-specific contact inputs. The local argument
does not use this compact step. None of the present statements
extends local uniqueness to singular klt domains or repairs
the pending local normalized-volume comparison. All forty-two
problem statuses and the general Siu, envelope, spectral and
quantization boundaries retain their prior scope.

\section{Relative entropy and normalized power energies}
\label{sec:provenance-relative-entropy-energy}

Section~\ref{sec:rf-relative-entropy-energy} imports known complex

The nineteen-page source was read in full, including references,

the entropy definition in Section~3, Lemma~2.5, Theorems~3.5
and~3.7, Lemmas~3.8--3.9 and Propositions~3.10 and~3.12.
The power estimate is on actual pages~10--13; the entropy-energy
argument is on pages~14--16 and the change-of-class argument
on pages~17--18. These locators are for the publisher file.

The book divides each non-pluripolar measure by its own positive
model mass $M_\phi$. The source's raw energy is
$E_r=M_\phi e_r$. On actual page~13 its exponential coefficient
contains $M_\phi^{1/n}$; multiplication by $E_r^{-1/n}$ cancels
that mass in the normalized power-weight estimate. The Young
inequality calculation consequently uses the probability density
$g=f/M_\phi$ and its entropy bound. It first applies the known
measure characterization to establish finite critical energy,
then uses its reciprocal to obtain the quantitative bound.
There is no preliminary assumption that an arbitrary full-mass
finite-energy measure has a smooth-volume density.

Selected dependency checks use \cite{DDL23RelativeV2},
arXiv:2303.11584v2, revised 3 October 2023. Theorem~1.4 is on

actual pages~53--55, printed pages~51--53. The repeated
characterization, Theorem~6.15, is on actual page~70, printed
page~68. These six actual pages were inspected for this import.
No complete rereading of the seventy-three-page survey is claimed.
In particular the scalar-weight current comparison is justified
through the source's approximation and plurifine localization,
not by taking a top wedge of a nonclosed scalar multiple.

The source's $\ddc_s=2\ddc_b$ is handled by
$\theta_s=2\theta_b$ with the same potentials. Raw measures
and raw energies both gain $2^n$, so probability measures,
normalized energies and entropy are unchanged. The result
concerns fixed smooth compact K\"ahler geometry and positive
model mass. The one-dimensional bounded-difference conclusion
does not become a higher-dimensional minimal-singularity claim.
Changing a smooth reference volume preserves finite entropy;
changing $\theta$ requires the positive-perturbation premise
in Proposition~3.12, except for the displayed direct
one-dimensional calculation. No general answer to the source's
Questions~1.1 or~3.11 is claimed.

The separately read Guedj--Lu arXiv:2502.02313v2 Orlicz paper
remains a research dependency in this checkpoint. Its weight

No general non-Archimedean Siu, closure, entropy comparison,

The import contains known results and identified direct formal

\section{Orlicz conditions in a fixed K\"ahler class}
\label{sec:provenance-orlicz-K}

Section~\ref{sec:ytd-orlicz-boundedness} uses the known classical
Ko\l odziej estimate and the reduction in
\cite{GuedjLu25FromYauV2}, arXiv:2502.02313v2, revised
18 February 2025. The complete fourteen-page source and all actual
pages were read and inspected in the preceding research phase.
This import reuses that reading. Theorem~A is on actual page~2;
Definition~1.5 and Example~1.6 are on actual page~4;
Lemma~2.2 and its proof are on actual pages~6--7;
Step~3 is on actual page~7, with Remark~2.3 on page~8;
Example~2.4 and Question~2.5 are on page~8 and the radial
integration calculation on page~9. The official arXiv history
was checked on 5 October 2026 and still lists v2 as its last revision.
The statements are not assigned a later journal pagination.

The probability density uses the volume of the fixed K\"ahler class.
An upper bound on the Luxembourg norm is used at a scale strictly
larger than that norm. The auxiliary weight is $1+w(t/R)$, so
its integral and normalization constant are bounded. A shifted
tail function preserves reciprocal integrability. This step does
not replace scaled Orlicz integrability by an unjustified integral
of $w(F)$ at scale one.

The source-page~7 calculation is written directly with the factors
retained. The low-density term is $e^Be^{-\alpha v}$;
for $b(x)=-\chi((B-e^x)/\alpha)$ the derivative has coefficient
$1/\alpha$, and the endpoint derivative involves
$\widehat h(\log B)$. The smooth minimum uses the proposed lower
barrier strictly above the potential at that point and a positive
auxiliary measure. These are explicit elementary calculations in
a known proof, not a new theorem or a counterexample to Theorem~A.
The import has fixed geometry and a K\"ahler reference form.
It does not import the fully nonlinear Theorem~B's uniformity in
its equation normalizer or a general Hermitian/big-class result.

The continuous-solvability assertion for the explicit
double-logarithmic power weight is checked separately in the archived
publisher PDF of \cite{Kol98}, Acta Math.\ 180 (1998), 69--117,
DOI 10.1007/BF02392879. The source has forty-nine actual pages.
Eight selected actual pages were freshly inspected: 5--6,
10--11 and 20--23, printed pages 73--74, 78--79 and 88--91.
The differential convention is on actual page~5, printed page~73;
condition (A) on actual pages~10--11, printed pages~78--79;
Theorem~2.4.2 on actual page~20, printed page~88;
the doubling condition (2.5.3) on actual page~22, printed page~90;
and Theorem~2.5.2 with Example~1 on actual page~23, printed page~91.
No full rereading of this paper is claimed. Its download filename
does not change the article's 1998 publication date.

For the explicit moment with order $q>n$, the original tail condition
is $\int^{\infty}dy/[yH(y)^{1/n}]<\infty$ with $H(y)$ comparable
to $(\log y)^q$. The logarithmic weight satisfies the source's
doubling condition. Smooth changes of the reference probability
volume preserve its integrability; continuity comes from the
original theorem, rather than an oscillation estimate alone.
The local radial example and its separate one-variable hypothesis
test order $n-1$. They do not solve Question~2.5 or supply an
unverified compact gluing assertion.

Guedj--Lu use $\ddc_s=2\ddc_b$ and Ko\l odziej uses
$\ddc_s=4\pi\ddc_b$. Multiplying the class form by the same
factor and keeping the potential scales both top measures
equally, preserving the probability density. Geometry constants
are not numerically equated. The earlier general big-class modulus
retains its explicit relative bound. The separate toric entropy
class-perturbation candidate remains a research draft with no
production receipt or theorem status. All canonical problem
statuses and general non-Archimedean Siu, closure, spectral and
A2--A4 boundaries retain their previous scope.

\section{Boundary singularities and a homogeneous solution family}
\label{sec:provenance-boundary-poisson}

Section~\ref{sec:ytd-boundary-poisson-nonuniqueness} explains the known

14 September 2026. The official version history was checked on

The complete fourteen-page file was read and all fourteen actual
pages displayed in seven sheets. Equation~(1.2) and its precise

The controlling construction is Section~3, pages~8--13:

On page~8 the source defines $p=e_1$ and writes the scalar component
of its Cayley map last, while claiming $P_{B,e_1}\circ C=\rho$.
With that component order the distinguished point is the last
coordinate vector. The book permutes the components, placing the
scalar component first, and checks both numerator and denominator
directly. This is a coordinate correction; the constructed family
and its mathematical properties are unchanged by the permutation.
The outer-product Hessian identity and pairwise nonproportionality
test are explicitly identified direct formal consequences of the
known construction. They are not original-result claims.

The source's operator coefficient is unnecessary for the homogeneous
zero equation: multiplication of $\ddc$ by a positive constant
preserves positivity and a zero top measure. The book keeps its
own operator throughout and makes no positive pole-mass comparison
from this example. Ordinary locally bounded Bedford--Taylor
noncharging of the analytic axis is essential; a calculation only
off that axis would be insufficient.

The import retains complex dimension at least two, continuity inside
the ball, boundary value zero away from the distinguished point,
and comparable nontangential growth. It does not impose the exact
curve-by-curve normalization used to define a Poisson envelope.
Wang's separate general-domain geometric characterization,
Theorem~1.1 on page~3, uses the prior Huang--Wang and Wang
geodesic-regularity papers in Section~2. Those dependencies have
not been independently acquired here; that general-domain theorem
is not imported into the book by this checkpoint.

There is no claim of a boundary mass, finite global energy,
critical Gibbs concentration, planar nonuniqueness or extension

retains absence of boundary blow-up. Canonical problem statuses,
the native catalogue, the research-only toric candidate and the
general Siu/closure and A2--A4 boundaries are preserved. No new
independent production-model receipt is claimed.

The algebraic formula concerns normal complex klt pairs
with effective rational boundaries and a finite crepant
map. It does not assume that the map is Galois or that
the singularity is isolated. Galois closure can introduce
negative crepant boundary coefficients. The acquired
proof therefore uses lifted degenerations and compatible
Reeb vectors rather than such a reduction.

\section{Differential forms and symplectic stable degeneration}

ring jets and Artin--Rees. In the symplectic application,

conjugacy. K-semistability is asserted for a choice of
minimizing Reeb vector, rather than every form-dilating
grading or every rational grading.

or import of its uniqueness-of-grading classification

and then uses local analyticity of \'etale maps.
No convergence of the given formal map, compatibility
with a prescribed symplectic form, or equivariant

The main source of this follow-up,

6 February 2026. Its PDF

formal-rigidity audit and fifty-six newly displayed.

Theorem~6.1 and its proof on pages~38--45 identify
polarized smoothability with projective symplectic
resolution in its projective setting. Theorem~6.3
and its proof on page~45 retain those hypotheses
and explicitly say that the approximating analytic

on page~53 isolates the conditional assumption of

new dependency-complete proof of general metric
existence. The hyperK\"ahler quotient applications
in Proposition~6.10 on pages~49--51 retain their
integrality, connectedness and regularity conditions
and are not imported for arbitrary quotients.

The scalar-grading caveat of Remark~6.17 on
page~53 also matters: a weight-two metric scaling
can have a finite kernel, whereas a faithful
algebraic action may give a different integral
weight for the form. Neither normalization forces
all symplectic minimizers to be divisorial.

\section{Nilpotent and hypertoric examples of minimizing gradings}
\label{sec:provenance-symplectic-examples}

The four subsections beginning at
\Cref{sec:ytd-local-nilpotent-minimizer} import known applications
and give a worked cyclic surface. They retain zero boundary and the
complex algebraic category. Xu--Zhuang with Guenancia's appendix
\cite{XuZhuang26StableFormsV2}, version~2, was completely read and
displayed in the earlier audit. Its Lemmas~7.1--7.3 and proofs are
on actual pages~32--34; Lemma~7.4 and Theorem~7.5 with proofs
on~35--36; Definition~7.7, Lemmas~7.8--7.9 and Theorem~7.10 with
proofs on~36--38. This checkpoint reuses that full-source record.
The nilpotent statement concerns the normalization of the orbit
closure. The hypertoric minimizer theorem does not assume the
unimodularity separately used for a smooth projective resolution.
Neither application's K-semistability is upgraded to general
K-polystability or unrestricted metric existence. The product
claim sketched with omitted details in Remark~7.11 is not imported.

Two small corrections are essential to reading those proofs.
The connected center, not the full center, is used: a semisimple
group can have a finite nontrivial center, and a torus action can
have a finite kernel. Effective image tori preserve the valuation
rays. The hypertoric coordinate swap satisfies
$\iota^*\sigma_Y=-\sigma_Y$, rather than preserving the form itself.
It does preserve its line, which is the automorphism group actually
used by the centrality argument. The chapter also makes the
Hamiltonians torus-invariant when explaining the isotropic-orbit
dimension bound. These repairs do not assert a counterexample to
the cited minimizing theorems.

The additional classical primary input
\cite{BrylinskiKostant94NilpotentPublished} is the published
JAMS~7(2) (1994), printed~269--298, not the shorter 1992
announcement. A thirty-page public copy of the printed journal
article was acquired after the publisher download returned an
access error. The journal header, title, authors and first and last
printed pages were checked against the journal metadata. Four
selected pages were displayed. Proposition~1.2 with its proof
is on actual page~3, printed~271; Theorem~7.1 with its proof is
on actual page~26, printed~294. The former treats homogeneous
covers; the application here uses the orbit itself. The latter
assumes nonzero projection to the active simple factors. Its
earlier proof dependencies are not claimed to have been read in
full, nor is an independent full verification of the 1994 paper
claimed.

Namikawa's first part
\cite{Namikawa25HypertoricCharacterizationV5} is the
forty-six-page arXiv version~5, revised 18 October 2025.
The separately published forty-nine-page edition is not used for
locators. Selected introduction, Hamiltonian preliminaries,
equidimensionality, quotient construction and final classification
sections were read; seventeen selected actual pages were displayed.
Lemma~1.1 and the local-model discussion are on pages~5--7;
Proposition~2.1 and its proof on~8--9; Example~3.5 on~22--23;
Corollary~5.4, Theorem~5.5 and Corollary~5.6 on~41--43;
Theorem~5.8, Remark~5.9 and Question~5.10 on~44.
The analytic classification and the source's proof mechanisms are
identified as known inputs; this selected reading is not described
as a complete dependency audit of the first part.

The six-page continuation
\cite{Namikawa26HypertoricCharacterizationIIV2}, version~2,
revised 6 April 2026, was read in full, including its proof and
bibliography, and all six actual pages were displayed. The main
theorem is unnumbered in Section~1, on pages~1--2; it is not
Theorem~2.1. Its proof occupies pages~2--5. The comparison requires
form weight two, a projective symplectic resolution and an effective
Hamiltonian half-dimensional torus commuting with the good action.
Changing that action by a cocharacter is allowed and can be necessary.
The principal-bundle comparison removes the degree-one resonance
in the Moser equation. Equidimensionality and normality justify
extension across the excluded locus. The torus-equivariant
algebraic comparison answers Question~5.10 with precisely these
hypotheses. The source's opposite form and Hamiltonian sign
conventions are kept explicit. No general classification without
the resolution or weight hypothesis is inferred.

The cyclic surface computations are direct weighted-hypersurface
and finite-degree consequences, not original results. The cover
has degree $q$ and normalized volume $4/q$. The weight-two action
has kernel of order $\gcd(q,2)$; passing to primitive weights also
changes the integral form weight. The local alpha value is
$2/\sqrt q$, whereas the critical Moser--Trudinger value is
$3/\sqrt q$ with the previously fixed energy convention. The
comparison among alternate weights is not presented as exhausting
all valuations. No new production-model receipt, original priority
claim or change in the canonical open-problem statuses follows.

\section{Fixed ideal errors and conditional entropy convergence}
\label{sec:provenance-fixed-ideal-error}

Section~\ref{sec:ch46-fixed-ideal-error} gives the elementary
graded/antigraded ideal argument of Boucksom's appendix,
\cite[Lemma~A.7]{Trusiani26}, followed by its conditional
measure-theoretic use. The controlling source is
arXiv:2605.30063v2, revised 17 August 2026 at 08:55:46 UTC.
All twenty-five actual PDF pages, extracted text, proof, appendix
and references were read and displayed in the special-Fujita source
review of 6 October. Lemma~A.7 and its proof are on actual~21;
Proposition~A.6 starts on~20 and its final fixed-error step is on~21.
The existing version-specific bibliography entry is retained.

The pointwise proof uses finite values of vertical ideals,
superadditivity, subadditivity, and a fixed shifted inclusion.
The strict range $m>m_0$ avoids an unstated zero-index convention.
The integrated conclusion separately assumes weak convergence,
a nonnegative discrepancy bound, continuity of the fixed model
function, and its zero integral against the limiting measure.
An entropy decomposition with continuous reference term is an
additional hypothesis. These are an imported argument and direct
formal consequences, not a new theorem or a complete proof of
special Fujita approximation or entropy regularization.

Fifty-five selected dependency pages were read and displayed:
DEL arXiv:\allowbreak math/0002035v2 on actual~1, 4--8 and~10--17;
Li arXiv:\allowbreak 2102.09457v1 on~1 and~4--13;
Li arXiv:\allowbreak 2001.01366v4 on~1 and~33--38;

and BJ arXiv:\allowbreak 2509.15016v1 on~1, 31--36 and~63--65.
None is represented as a full dependency-paper reading.
Their complete Calabi--Yau, envelope, support and weighted analytic
antecedents remain outside this selected audit. The archived BFJ
file uses author prepublication pagination, and Li's geodesic paper
is distinct from his Fujita paper.

The source review records the invalid scalar-supremum pairing with

step, intermediate canonical-pullback convention and relative

\section{Fixed divisor traces and the canonical reduction}
\label{sec:provenance-fixed-divisor-traces}

Section~\ref{sec:ch46-fixed-divisor-traces} gives the fixed-divisor
step of \cite[Lemma~4.3]{Trusiani26} with explicit lower and upper
volume bounds. The controlling Trusiani v2 statement is on
actual~13 and its proof on~14; both belong to the preceding complete
twenty-five-page reading. The exposition uses the Morse estimate
in \cite[Proposition~3.3]{BFJ09} and the volume derivative in its
Theorem~A. The published author-hosted BFJ offprint was checked
on actual~1--3, 12--20 and~23--28 in the earlier selected source
review. Proposition~3.3 is on printed~295 (actual~17); the proof
of the volume derivative is on printed~295--296 (actual~17--18).
Its external reference for the elementary algebraic Morse proof
is not represented as a fresh full book reading.

The argument first fixes an ample test divisor and a small positive
parameter, then takes the approximant limit, and only afterwards
takes the parameter to zero. A fixed Cartier divisor is written
as a difference of ample divisors. This yields trace convergence
without a scalar-supremum formula for every effective divisor.
The latter formula, printed in Trusiani actual~7 and Li--Fujita
actual~6, was diagnosed in the special-Fujita source review and
is not used here.

The relative canonical reduction is stated on smooth birational
models. Fixed-divisor trace convergence does not remove the
varying relative discrepancy intersection, identify entropy or
certify a complete repaired special-Fujita proof. Canonical
pullbacks on a normal intermediate model need their own
definition or a justified Weil-cycle projection. Chapter~68
retains the separate approximation and entropy inputs. This is
an imported trace argument and its direct canonical reduction,
with no new original theorem or verification receipt. All canonical
problem statuses and the general Siu/closure boundary retain
their scope.

\section{Continuous Hessian domination and conditional regularity}
\label{sec:provenance-continuous-hessian-domination}

Section~\ref{sec:ytd-continuous-hessian-domination} gives the known
continuous global domination argument in
\cite{GuedjLu23HessianV1}, arXiv:\allowbreak 2302.03354v1, submitted
7 February 2023 at 09:55:38 UTC. All fifteen actual pages were read
and displayed, including the references. Smooth decreasing approximation
is on actual~4--5, the continuous operator and contact inequality
on~5--7, positive mass on~8, and domination and exponential comparison
on~9. The publisher PDF returned HTTP~403; the twenty-four-page 2025
journal edition was not acquired or reviewed. The preprint's
$\ddc=2\rmi\partial\bar\partial$ converts a book potential
$\varphi$ to source potential $\varphi/(4\pi)$ for the same forms.
The exponential coefficient is adjusted accordingly; domination
depends on the strict sublevel factor, not a preferred unit coefficient.

The difference-of-powers display in the positive-mass proof has an
incorrect upper power and omits a fixed factor. The ordinary identity
used in the argument is
\[
 A^j\wedge B^{m-j}-A^m
   =(B-A)\wedge
       \sum_{\ell=0}^{m-j-1}A^{j+\ell}\wedge B^{m-j-1-\ell}.
\]
The source review records this literal algebra issue and the fixed
reference metric in the stability proof. Neither is represented as
a counterexample to the main theorem or a complete repaired external
proof chain. The envelope contact-support and mixed-current results
retain their cited primary antecedents.

The local theory and its broader smooth approximation are checked
in the published \cite{GuNguyen18DirichletPublished}: actual~1--17
and~50--53, corresponding to printed~1189--1205 and~1238--1241.
Only those twenty-one pages were read and displayed. The bounded
operator and local comparison use the locally conformal K\"ahler
reference metric fixed on actual~6; Remark~3.19 on~17 extends
smooth approximation to general Hermitian metrics by a different
maximality input. The printed expansion (3.5) uses $n-k$ where its
binomial sign and smooth remainder power require $m-k$. The
$\ddc T$ display on actual~10 also omits derivative terms from
nonclosed background factors; the later estimates explicitly retain
background torsion. The source's
$\ddc=\rmi\partial\bar\partial/\pi$ corresponds to half the book
potential. No full-paper or full local Dirichlet proof review is claimed.

Two further pages of \cite{KN16WeakHessianV2},
arXiv:\allowbreak 1507.06755v2, revised 10 March 2016 at
11:24:27 UTC, were read and displayed on actual~12--13.
They contain Theorem~2.16, its Lemma~2.17 and their local proofs.
The continuous uniformly approximable zero-Hessian potential and
the merely bounded competitor have distinct roles. Ten other

$\varepsilon\phi_\varepsilon$ family.

and its explicit erratum

The original has twenty-eight actual pages, of which

regularization proof on~10--12. Finite prescribed
mass and a zero-boundary subsolution in
$\mathcal E_m^0$ remain explicit. The original's

An additional quantitative issue occurs in the
original Proposition~2.20 on actual~14--15.
If $c$ denotes capacity and $f=c^{1/m}$, the
extinction scale in its displayed recursion is
proportional to $c(0)^{(\tau-1)/m}$, whereas the
next printed display uses $c(0)^{\tau-1}$.
The erratum still invokes this proposition.
The book does not admit the resulting printed
numerical exponent for $m>1$. This is a diagnostic
of the displayed derivation; it is not a
counterexample to the main qualitative H\"older
theorem or a certification of its complete repaired
quantitative proof. Weighted H\"older factors,
wedge degree, mass powers and Jensen regions also
retain their precise source-review corrections.

The entire acquired fifteen-page PDF of
\cite{Zeriahi20ModulusV1} was read and displayed.
ArXiv metadata dates the only listed v1 submission
to 16 July 2020 at 15:20:28 UTC; the pinned PDF
internally prints 27 November 2024 on actual~1.
No 2024 submission or revision is inferred from
that internal date. Actual~9--12 were already
read from the identical hashed PDF in the earlier
ample-locus audit and were reread here.

The literal Lemma~3.3 on actual~10 claims a
uniform oscillation modulus on shrinking interior
collars without boundary control. On the unit disk,
the bounded harmonic function $\operatorname{Im}
\log(1-z)$ has zero radial-mean error, while its
oscillation at $x=1-a\delta$, with fixed $a>B$,
is at least $\arctan(1/(2a))$ on the
radius-$\delta$ ball. Thus that boundary-free
uniform assertion is not used. The nested-ball
proof on actual~11--12 gives the fixed-compact
consequence when its starting radius is chosen
below the positive interior buffer. Theorem~3.2
on~9--10 separately includes a boundary modulus.
The existing logarithmic ample-locus passage now
states this buffer and the dependence of its
constant. A local background must dominate the
metric when making a potential subharmonic.

All four PDFs were read and displayed:
seventy-seven physical pages, including four
reused from the identical hashed file.
Fourteen source diagnostics distinguish false
assertions, repairable formulas and cleared objections.
This account explains known qualitative inputs.
It admits no original result, production receipt
or canonical solution; the remaining quantitative
and general Siu/closure statements retain their

\label{sec:provenance-tau-moments}

\cite{Wang26TauV1}. The exact source is
arXiv:\allowbreak 2610.04994v1, submitted 4 October 2026 at
06:29:10 UTC. All fifteen actual pages were read and displayed,
including proofs and references. The lower bound is Theorem~1.1
on actual~2 with proof on~11; the dual-lattice inequality is

reductions are on~8--10; the cubic construction and its finite

normalized tau constant is still open.

The exact published PDF of
\cite{dJS23TropicalMomentsPublished}, \emph{Canadian Journal of
Mathematics}~75(4) (2023), 1045--1075, was read and displayed in
all thirty-one actual pages. The edge tau formula and moment
identity are Theorems~11.2 and~11.4 on actual~28--29. Its auxiliary
random-tree calculations require two local corrections. The
denominator in the first proof display of Theorem~5.4 on actual~14
is $L_f$, not the printed $L_e$, in a sum over $f$. The auxiliary
incident-edge sum must exclude $f=e$: with the zero-diagonal
matrix $Z$ on~17, $AZ\delta_e=t_0$, where $A$ is that paper's
unsigned vertex--edge incidence matrix and
$t_0(p,e)=\sum_{f\ne e,\,f\ni p}P(e,f)$. Including the diagonal
as in Definition~5.2(ii) adds $P(e)(\delta_u+\delta_v)$ and
contradicts the displayed Theorem~5.4 already for one unit edge.
The later separate diagonal terms retain their role. The endpoint
resistance interpolation in Example~4.6 on~11 also presupposes
that its source is outside the open edge; subdivision at the
source provides the convention used in the tau formula.
These diagnostics do not assert that the final moment identity
is false. Further external tiling and volume antecedents are
not completely audited here.

All twenty actual pages of
\cite{dJS22RayleighPublished}, \emph{Rocky Mountain Journal of
Mathematics}~52(4) (2022), 1403--1422, were read and displayed
from the publisher PDF supplied by NSF PAR. Proposition~5.2
on~9 permits any generalized inverse of the positive Laplacian;
Theorem~7.8 on~15 proves the cycle projection formula without
the later contraction theorem. Remark~5.3 on~9 needs centering:
if $L_q$ is the grounded inverse padded with zeros, then
$Q^+=H(|V|^{-1}\sum_q L_q)H$, with
$H=\mathrm{Id}-\mathbf1\mathbf1^T/|V|$. The uncentered average
has the same zero-mass pairing but need not equal $Q^+$ as a matrix.

The literal arbitrary-support version of Theorem~8.2 on~18--19
also needs a restriction: both discrete measures must be supported
outside the contracted open edge for its single-edge rank-one
formula. For $\Gamma=[0,2]$, $e=[0,1]$ and
$\nu=\delta_{1/4}-\delta_{3/4}$, the quotient energy is zero
but the displayed formula gives $1/4$. Interior charges require
subdivision followed by successive contractions. This does not
affect the vertex-supported formula or the earlier projection
theorem used by Wang. The resistance-only loop removal in the
moments paper is distinct from the loop-retaining metric-lattice
reduction in \eqref{eq:ch08-tau-uniform-bound}.

\section{Finite generation and the base-locus erratum}
\label{sec:provenance-ELMNP-fans}

Section~\ref{sec:appC-finite-generation-fans} uses the corrected
Proposition~1.1 and Lemma~2.1 of
\cite{ELMNP23BaseLocusErratumV1}, exactly
arXiv:\allowbreak 2309.16722v1, submitted 20 September 2023 at
22:21:25 UTC. All seven actual pages were read and displayed, including
the two convex-geometric arguments, the proposition proof and references.
The statement is on actual~1, the normal-fan proof on~2--3, and the
valuation/Veronese proof on~5--6. Its ambient category is an integral
reduced finite-type variety over an algebraically closed field.
The same fan works for all valuation costs; the integer is chosen after
the smooth refinement. Both sides of the product identity are integrally
closed. These qualifications are part of the imported statement.

The original \cite{ELMNP06BaseLociPublished} is the published
\emph{Annales de l'Institut Fourier}~56(6) (2006), 1701--1734,
DOI~10.5802/aif.2225. All 35 physical pages were read and displayed,
including the publisher cover. Physical~2 is printed~1701.
Proposition~4.7 and Lemma~4.8 are on actual~28; the old induction is
on~29--30. Its repeated refinement did not establish termination in
dimensions at least three. The erratum replaces that proof. The
original parenthetical arbitrary-Noetherian-scheme scope is broader
than the category explicitly treated in the corrected proof and is not
certified here. Section~1's augmented-base-locus definition is independent
of that induction: Definition~1.2 and Proposition~1.5 are on actual~7--8.

Two literal errors in the optional Remark~2.3 on erratum actual~4 are
separate from its normal-fan proof. The relation must read
$\sum_i b_i v_i=0$, rather than the printed
$\sum_i b_i\lambda_i=0$. In the reduction
$\lambda'_i=\lambda_i-tb_i$, coordinates outside the support of $b$
must be retained, rather than set to zero. Rational two-dimensional
examples show that either printed error can change the target vector.
The normal-fan proof on~2--3 does not use those formulas.
After (7) on actual~6, the product inclusion has degree $dm$,
rather than the printed $m$; the displayed valuation chain itself
has the correct degree. These diagnostics do not refute Lemma~2.1
or corrected Proposition~1.1.

The original source also needs several local reading qualifications.
In Example~1.11 on actual~9, the negative part $N$, rather than the
arbitrary effective part $E$ of an ample decomposition, has support
in $\operatorname{Null}(P)$. Definition~2.9 on~17 uses a proper
irreducible center for a nontrivial discrete valuation.

positive hyperplane ray inside the big cone of the one-point blow-up

\section{Regular polynomial dynamics and the hybrid metric interface}
\label{sec:provenance-hybrid-polynomial}

Section~\ref{sec:hybrid-polynomial-escape} explains a conditional
application of the already proved projective hybrid metric theorem.
It admits no new original result and does not certify the recent
non-Archimedean dynamical proof. The approximation and identification
inputs remain explicit in
\eqref{eq:hybrid-escape-uniformity-input} and
\eqref{eq:hybrid-escape-rank-identification}; closed support is needed
for the affine passage. Degree division, reference subtraction,
positive hybrid scaling, semipositivity and special-fibre multiplicities
are retained before taking a measure.

The new source is exactly \cite{Irokawa26RegularV1},
arXiv:\allowbreak 2610.06821v1, submitted 5 October 2026 at
17:56:42 UTC. Its internal PDF date is 30 September 2026.
All 23 physical pages, including references, were read as complete
extracted text and displayed as original page images. The main hybrid
weak limit is Theorem~1.1 on actual~2 and Theorem~6.1 on~20.
The central maximum/product identification is Theorem~4.4 on~13;
the rank argument is on~14 and the affine uniformity argument of
Proposition~5.2 is on~18--19. A full reading is not certification
of those proofs or of their complete dependency chain.

The last uniformity argument contains a concrete failure of its
chosen covering constants. Let
$f_t(x,y)=(y,ty^2-x)$ for real $0<t<1/2$; its inverse is
$(tx^2-y,x)$. Both are regular of degree two, with $l_+=l_-=1$.
The source's maximum of one and the absolute values of map coefficients
is $C(t)=1$, hence its choices give $\delta=\varepsilon=1$.
At $z=(0,2)$ both image norms equal~2, strictly less than
$\delta\|z\|^2=4$, while $\|z\|>\varepsilon^{-1}=1$.
Thus neither of its asserted covering sets contains $z$.
This refutes that intermediate covering with those constants, not
the main weak-convergence theorem. The actual constants in
\cite{Kawaguchi13RegularPublished} also control Nullstellensatz
certificates; the complex proof retains monomial counts. The
product-domain telescoping in \cite{Lee12RegularPairV2} additionally
uses forward invariance. A complete replacement of the recent
uniformity proof has not been certified here.

Other literal interfaces of the new v1 need correction. Its first
pullback displays on actual~1 and~10 use reciprocal degrees, whereas
Section~4.3 on~15 has the correct multiplying degrees.
Proposition~3.5 on~9 omits the positive hybrid factor used correctly
on~7. The model measure on~8 omits the special-fibre multiplicities
declared on~7. The same page's arbitrary-model regularization wording
omits the semipositivity required by its cited BE21 construction.
Chart ideal generators on~7 need coordinate functions, rather than
only base-ring coefficients. On~11 the required leading-term common-zero
assertion is at infinity; an affine fixed zero disproves the broader
printed assertion. Several entry-time indices and bounds on~12--14
do not supply a continuity certificate as written.

The rank proof on actual~14 selects a first coordinate from a nonzero
tuple without establishing its invertibility on the whole compact set.
A product of two H\'enon maps, with the first block fixed at zero
and the second escaping, separates these assertions. After local
normalization, the constant~1 in a truncated maximum is an extra
function unless a late-escape estimate makes it inactive. The
Nullstellensatz coefficient dependence on~16 requires rational
functions/nonzero minors in general, not the claimed linear sums.
On~20 the affine hybrid projection is incorrectly called proper.
On~21 the raw degree model function is identified with an affine
normalized potential without the degree/reference correction.
The cutoff in the final projective test integral on~22 must be
retained as an extension by zero. These diagnostics explain the

For the earlier source, exactly \cite{Irokawa23HenonV2}, all

\section{Sequential rational dynamics: separate continuity interfaces}
\label{sec:provenance-hybrid-sequential}

Section~\ref{sec:hybrid-sequential-rational} uses the exact
\cite{FavreGong26SequentialV4}, arXiv:\allowbreak 2406.15892v4.
The arXiv revision is 20 June 2026 at 03:27:01 UTC; the internal
PDF date is 23 June 2026. All 58 physical pages, including references,
were read and displayed in the preceding source checkpoint.
The published 2025 journal record is distinct metadata; its full
published proof is not claimed freshly checked here. This integration
reuses the frozen reading and admits no new original theorem.

The Banach construction is in Section~3, measure continuity is
Theorem~5.8 on actual~47--49, intrinsic Lyapunov continuity is
Corollary~5.9 on~50, and the Luo comparison is in Section~6
on~50--56. Twenty-seven source diagnostics distinguish literal
auxiliary failures from the unrefuted main continuity assertions.
Reading the entire preprint is not certification of its proofs
or their complete dependency chain.

The source's resultant scale on actual~30 has an incorrect printed
exponential identity. Equation~\eqref{eq:hybrid-sequential-resultant-scale}
retains the positive scale and its correct value
$R_n^{\varepsilon_n}=e^{-1}C_d^{\varepsilon_n}$.
The boundary criterion is potential good reduction: a singleton Julia
set need not be at the original Gauss point. Weak Berkovich convergence
and strong path distance remain different topologies. For example,
points in infinitely many distinct residue branches at a fixed
positive path distance can converge weakly to the Gauss point.
Rumely's minimum set lies on a specified finite tree; that does not
make the ambient infinite-branch metric balls compact.

Several construction formulas need local or ultrafilter qualifications.
The subsequence argument requires a nonprincipal ultrafilter.
The characteristic-set complement in the spectrum identification
must be used consistently. Quotient representatives bounded on an
ultrafilter-large set require modification on its complement before
they belong to the globally bounded product ring. Coordinatewise
root lifting holds after discarding an ultrafilter-small set, not
necessarily at every coordinate. Comparable positive scales need
not have ratio at most one, and conjugacy comparisons require the
same sequence of conjugacy classes. A general projective point over
a ring may correspond to an invertible quotient rather than one
globally unimodular pair; the actual normalized tuples over
$A_\varepsilon$ supply the needed pairs in this application.

A quasi-model section tuple must be nonzero on each relevant fibre
before its logarithm has a finite integral. Arbitrary data that
vanish identically on one fibre give the constant $-\infty$ there.
The critical section of a characteristic-zero degree-$d$ map is
nonzero, but that fact cannot repair the broader unrestricted
quasi-model wording. Basepoint-free model functions over arbitrary
rings are not automatically dense: over a trivially valued field
their indicated degree-zero relative weights can all vanish.
The actual product-ring construction has appropriate norm constants
and a more specific test class.

The auxiliary assertion that bounded families of continuous functions
are compact fails on $\beta\mathbf N$: characteristic functions of
successive tails have a discontinuous pointwise limit. A displayed
difference-of-maxima identity and the claimed linearity of a
homogeneous polynomial norm also fail elementary tests. The
source's Fubini--Study density, chordal disk radius and powered
reference-correction display require the normalizations recorded
in the editorial review. These failures are not counterexamples
to the uniform Green-tail mechanism
\eqref{eq:hybrid-sequential-Green-tail}.

The singular step of Theorem~5.8 on actual~49 requires more than
common reduction of several quotient polynomials. For
$P_{1,n}=z$ and $P_{2,n}=z-a_n$, with $a_\omega=0$, the reductions
agree, but at $z=0$ one finite logarithm is $-\infty$ while their
maximum is finite. Residual-factor domination does not give the
claimed estimate for each original polynomial, and common residual
zeros need not be avoidable. Proper nonzero data, continuous
truncations and a uniform integrability bound such as
\eqref{eq:hybrid-sequential-critical-uniform-integrability}
are a sufficient separate interface; a complete repair of the
preprint's singular maximum argument is not certified.

The derivative on actual~50 must use $|J/d|$ rather than
$(1/d)|J|$. On $\mathbf C^e$ the intrinsic exponent for $z^d$
is $e\log d$; on the Robinson Gauss fibre it is zero.
Equation~\eqref{eq:hybrid-sequential-valued-derivative} preserves
this scaling. Möbius changes of reference add a continuous
relative-weight correction. The asymptotic-cone relation uses
scaled distance tending to zero. In the two-sided scale comparison,
an upper bound on $-\log R_n/r_n$ does not itself give the positive
lower bound. That direction uses the optimized boundary map's
failure of potential good reduction and a preimage separated from
the Gauss point. No complete certification of both inequalities,
or of the final coordinate-conjugation proof, is added here.

The selected dependency scopes are exact arXiv editions:
\cite{Rumely13ResultantV2}, actual~1--3 and~13--14 of 37 pages;
\cite{Luo21LimitsV4}, actual~1--3,~22--23 and~28--31 of 41 pages;
\cite{Luo21TreesV2}, actual~1--2 and~16--24 of 49 pages; and
\cite{Poi25DynamicsZV5}, actual~1,~3--6,~13 and~38--41 of 43 pages.
These 35 physical pages were read and displayed, but none of the
four dependencies was read in full. The Luo selections include the

Poineau's Theorem~A on actual~3 and Theorem~6.26 on~39--41 concern

Definition~2.3 on~13 includes local arc connectedness; it does not

The read uniform test estimate explains the order of approximation,

\section{K3 dynamics: symmetric normalization and family inputs}
\label{sec:provenance-hybrid-K3}

Section~\ref{sec:hybrid-K3-eigen-currents} combines known fixed-fibre

Its main recent source is exactly \cite{Irokawa24K3V1},
arXiv:\allowbreak 2405.12517v1, submitted 21 May 2024.
All eighteen physical pages, including references, were read and
displayed in the preceding K3 checkpoint. Theorem~1.1 is the main
hybrid measure assertion; nineteen diagnostics identify failures
or missing interfaces without refuting the correctly defined limit.

The decisive check is on actual~11--13. Both directions of the
curvature approximation require $\Lambda^{-n}$ for positive $n$.
A signed-index rule that grows in the backward direction cannot
converge to the backward eigen-current. The raw mixed pullbacks
have exponentially growing mass; a tensor-degree denominator alone
does not normalize them. The explicit intersection calculation
\eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-approximant-mass} and probability normalization
\eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-invariant-probability} are known fixed-fibre
consequences of the two eigenclasses, not a new dynamical theorem.
\cite[Corollary~5.6.8]{Filip19K3V1} already retains both decaying
factors and gives a positive nonzero invariant mixed measure.

The primary Cantat source is the published \emph{Acta Mathematica}
187 (2001), pages~1--57, \cite{Cantat01K3Published}, not the date
suggested by the hosting filename. Actual~1,~7--8,~11--15 and~18--24
were read and displayed: fifteen physical pages. Theorem~2.4 on
actual~13--15 concerns the eigen-currents; continuous local potentials
also require Theorem~3.1 on~18--21. The invariant measure discussion
is in Section~3.3 on~22. Its further laminarity, entropy and complete
dependency chain were not freshly audited.

For Filip, exactly \cite{Filip19K3V1}, actual~1--4,~14--15 and~27--38
of the 59-page preprint were read and displayed: eighteen physical
pages. The general Picard-torsor lift is in Section~2.2.11 on~14--15.
Theorem~5.6.4 on~35 gives the fixed-homogeneity contraction;
Theorem~5.6.5 on~36--37 handles changing homogeneity on compact
torsor sets; Theorem~5.6.7 on~37 proves positivity; and
Corollary~5.6.8 on~38 gives the mixed measure.
The setup requires characteristic zero also in the residue field.
The real Picard group need not be the whole real $(1,1)$-cohomology.
Only the separate 2019 journal metadata was checked; its full
published PDF was not obtained, and no published-proof audit is
claimed.

The five selected pages~5--9 of Demailly's author-hosted Trento
layout were also read and displayed. They fix the differential
normalization, integration by parts, locally bounded
Bedford--Taylor products, the local Chern--Levine--Nirenberg estimate
and the uniform-convergence corollary with its proof. In that layout
$dd^c=i\partial\bar\partial/\pi$, twice the book's operator.
An ordinary logarithmic metric weight uses this curvature, while
the book's squared-norm weight uses the book's $dd^c$.
The later monotone theorem was only located, not read in full.

The hybrid v1 also omits special-fibre multiplicities in a model
measure. A nonreduced SNC fibre can have weighted generic degree~4
while its unweighted component sum is~3. No choice of differential
normalization removes this discrepancy. The chart ideal potential
uses a maximum of generator norms, not an infimum. Uniform approximation
by model functions alone does not establish semipositivity; the
models or approximation class must carry the appropriate positivity.

The family argument retains the reference curvature and the full
integration-by-parts cutoff terms. A claimed hybrid factor with
the wrong sign cannot be used in a positive approximation estimate.
Bounds at a single fixed iterate do not supply a uniform recurrent
tail. Changing homogeneous characters have to be tracked on compact
torsor or chart domains; an unrestricted global bound is not supplied
by Filip's fixed-field theorem. These are the reasons for the common
reference and quantifier requirements in
\eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-uniformity-input}.

The non-Archimedean mixed product also requires the actual continuous
potential class. The ample hybrid Monge--Amp\`ere theorem does not
immediately cover arbitrary real nef eigenclasses. A valid
polarization or ample-perturbation passage and uniform mixed-product
estimates are explicit inputs to
\eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-conditional-limit}. Neither the source's theorem
assertion nor the corrected fixed-fibre mass computation establishes
those family inputs. A complete repair of Theorem~1.1 is not certified.
