\section{Smooth Hessian integral comparison and its operator boundary}
\label{sec:provenance-Hessian-integral-comparison}

\Cref{sec:appH-smooth-integral-comparison} imports a local smooth
comparison mechanism and the independently published obstruction
for a product of eigenvalue sums. It is linked to the smooth
Hermitian estimate discussion in
\Cref{sec:ytd-direct-gradient-compactness}, without replacing that
discussion's background-dependent analytic inputs. This addition
promotes no original result and supplies no weak non-Archimedean
operator or general Siu/envelope repair.

The exact recent source is
\cite{DinewMygaSroka26IntegralV1}, submitted 5 October 2026,
13:30:52 UTC. All twelve physical pages of arXiv:2610.06313v1
were read as complete text and displayed. Its Definition~1.1,
actual~2, uses unweighted Lebesgue integrals. Definitions~2.1
and~2.7, actual~5--7, specify an open connected symmetric
eigenvalue domain and linearized ellipticity; they impose no
maximal-domain condition. A definition of decreasing weak
admissibility is not itself a nonlinear weak-measure construction.

The sufficiency argument is (3.2) and Remark~3.2, actual~8--9.
It uses the whole segment of Hessians and its elliptic
linearization. Its Proposition~3.1, actual~7, states the
Newton-divergence identity for any smooth symmetric matrix
field, but that wording omits the derivative symmetry. In the
acquired \cite{GuanCIMECourseNotes}, Lemma~2.1 on physical
and note pages~5--6 requires a Codazzi tensor. All six initial
pages of this forty-two-page course-hosted layout were read
and displayed, including that proof. It was compiled from
2012 CIME lectures and is not identified with the pagination
of the 2014 published chapter. Euclidean Hessians satisfy the
required Codazzi identity. The matrix-field calculation in
\Cref{sec:appH-Newton-segment} explains why the assumption
cannot be omitted. The complex analogue uses the holomorphic
and antiholomorphic Newton identities in the recent source's
Remark~3.3. The book states the real flux formula only for
smooth pairs with regular comparison boundary, avoiding an
unsupported Sard reduction for arbitrary C2 functions.

Theorem~1.2 and Corollary~1.3, actual~3, omit an additive
constant without imposing a normalization. Both integrals
change equally when that constant is added; the necessity
proof itself ends with an affine function, actual~9.
Proposition~1.4 additionally asserts equality with a G\"arding
cone without requiring maximality. Restriction to a proper
elliptic subcone preserves comparison. These literal range
issues are not a refutation of an appropriately normalized,
branch-qualified classification.

Remark~2.9 says only that F is differentiable, whereas the
pointwise expansion (3.7) differentiates its invariant
factor three times. The acquired
\cite{Ball84SymmetricPublished} is an author-hosted twenty-nine-image
scan. Its first ten physical pages, printed~699--708, were
read as OCR checked against the controlling images; the
nineteen remaining images were not reviewed. Theorem~3.2
and Corollary~3.3 on printed~705 distinguish derivative loss
at multiple eigenvalues from smooth factorization. The
simple-spectrum coordinate discussion on printed~706
preserves the given regularity; it creates no missing derivatives.
No full Ball proof review or C1 classification repair is claimed.

Two displayed real-jet formulas also require correction.
The quadratic in (3.8), actual~8, has Hessian 2D, rather
than D. For its real quartic, diagonal fourth derivatives
have coefficient three and off-diagonal ones coefficient
one. The printed (3.9), actual~9, uses ratio two. An
explicit coefficient comparison is needed before inferring
the vanishing in (3.10). The complex quartic in Remark~3.3
has diagonal ratio two and cannot inherit the real correction.
The simple-spectrum Jacobian in Lemma~3.4, actual~9--10,
has the expected Vandermonde invertibility; finite symbolic
checks do not settle the missing global regularity argument.

The example selection is exact. Example~4.1, actual~10,
gives the convex positive component of
\(\sigma_3-\sigma_1\) in complex dimension four.
Example~4.2 on that page compares quadratics on two
different real elliptic components; it does not contradict
the convex connected-branch sufficiency. Example~4.3,
actual~11, has a star-shaped nonconvex locus for
\(\sigma_3+\sigma_1\). Both later examples occur in this v1.
Its product-positivity notation (2.3), actual~5--6, must be
restricted to the positive-sum branch, rather than the
entire set on which a product happens to be positive.

The product-operator obstruction does not rely on the
recent classification. We read the entire published
\cite{Dinew22SadullaevPublished}: sixteen physical PDF
pages including one publisher cover and the research pages
995--1009. Its smooth p-plane positivity convention is
on printed~996. Theorem~4.1 is on printed~1004--1008;
Claims~4.3--4.4 supply the large-diagonal perturbation
and radial-family bounds on the exponent. The proof
outline in \Cref{thm:appH-Dinew-product-comparison}
retains both bounds. Remark~4.5's radial-only statement
does not have universal-pair scope. The viscosity approach
mentioned on printed~998 is not excluded by failure of
unweighted integral comparison.

Two narrow published typos are avoided: the comparison
display on printed~1004 repeats v on both sides, and the
invariant range on printed~1000 is written only through m,
despite the same paragraph's
\(\mathcal M_2=\sigma_1\sigma_2-\sigma_3\) in dimension three.
The book uses all n invariants and the comparison orientation
fixed by the source's question and proof. Its unrelated
A/B-classification argument is not imported here.

The primary-source checkpoint totals forty-four physical
pages in twenty-two displayed sheets and twelve elementary
formula/scope diagnostics. This integration reuses that
frozen evidence and adds no new primary-page reading,
independent model call or production receipt. The smooth
coordinate/form identity
\eqref{eq:appH-coordinate-Hessian-form} uses the book's
\(\ddc=\rmi\partial\bar\partial/(2\pi)\) and retains
its binomial and volume factors. Constant and mixed-degree
operators remain distinct from raw quotients. No canonical
problem status, general quotient weak measure, arbitrary
Hermitian integral principle or BJ22 homogeneous Siu
division conclusion follows from this addition.

\section{Positive entropy in hyper-K\"ahler families and the relative-map boundary}
\label{sec:provenance-HK-group-entropy}

\Cref{sec:hybrid-HK-dense-entropy} records the dense existence
statement of \cite[Theorem~1.1]{DenisiEtAl26EntropyV1}, exact
arXiv:2610.05173v1, submitted 4 October 2026, 12:31:16 UTC.
All eight physical pages were read as complete text and displayed.
The H2-level entropy convention is on actual~1; Corollary~1.3
is on actual~2 and its local-disk argument on actual~6--7.
The full proof is not independently certified by this integration.
The statement is attributed with polarization, projectivity,
non-isotriviality and $b_2\geq6$ retained. Its maps need not
form a chosen relative automorphism. Remark~1.5's additional
Zariski-dense generic-orbit conclusion uses a separate external
result and is not imported as Euclidean orbit density or
equidistribution.

The entire twenty-page \cite{DenisiEtAl26GroupsV1} and
fourteen-page \cite{AmerikSoldatenkovVerbitsky26V1} were also
read and displayed in their exact v1 layouts. The former's
Theorem~1.1 and proof on actual~10--13 retain a finite
exceptional set. A very general positive-entropy element may
be replaced by a power in its finite-index common subgroup;
the element itself need not belong to that subgroup. Example~1.4(1)
on actual~3 removes finite exceptions from its fourfold family.
The later entropy preprint's Example~1.6 says all members have
positive-entropy maps. That wording requires an already restricted
family or a further argument for the excluded parameters; the
cited density theorem alone does not remove them.

For the cone input, six selected pages, actual~22--27, of
\cite{Markman11SurveyV3} were read. Theorem~6.18 and
Lemma~6.23 distinguish the birational monodromy subgroup from
its image in $O(\operatorname{NS})$. If
$\rho:\operatorname{Mon}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}\to
O(\operatorname{NS})$ and $\Gamma=\operatorname{im}\rho$,
then $\Gamma$ has finite index in $O^+(\operatorname{NS})$,
the restriction kernel belongs to the birational subgroup,
and the chamber stabilizer is its image inside $\Gamma$.
It must not be identified without qualification with the full
ambient orthogonal-group stabilizer, as in the entropy preprint's
actual~3 wording. Finite kernels and finite indices remain
part of the passage from geometric groups to lattice actions.

The first ten of thirteen pages of \cite{Oguiso05EntropyV5}
were read, including Theorem~2.1 and its complete displayed
proof on actual~4--8. That theorem assumes an infinite group
all of whose elements have spectral radius one, and gives a
fixed primitive isotropic direction and virtual free abelian
rank at most $r-2$. The earlier Theorem~1.3 omits the infinite
qualifier from its rank bound; a trivial group at Picard rank
one cannot have rank at most $-1$. The infinite-group input
used here retains its own hypothesis.

Eight selected pages of \cite{Nikulin95ReflectionV6} include
Definition~1.1.2 and Theorem~1.1.3 on actual~5--7. Its
parabolic reflective lattice finiteness is modulo similarity
and isomorphism. Passing to finitely many actual Picard
lattices also uses the fixed deformation type's negative
square bound, as in the recent sources' proofs. The full
Picard lattice contains the positive polarization; its
orthogonal negative part is a different lattice. The entropy
preprint's actual~4 and ASV's actual~11 wording must retain
this distinction. Huybrechts' Remark~1.1, printed~231 of
\cite{Huybrechts18PolarizedPublished}, is the specified
embedding/vector finiteness input; only physical~1--6 of that
twenty-page published PDF were read. These are not readings
of the published Nikulin or Springer Markman layouts.

ASV's strong-density proof, actual~9--12, uses a homogeneous
dynamics input from the published Amerik--Verbitsky article.
The acquired \cite{AmerikVerbitsky16AuthorOrbits} is instead
the complete thirteen-page author version~0.9 of 13 April
2016, with the earlier parabolic-orbit title. It calls the
stabilizer of a nonisotropic negative vector a parabolic
subgroup, although that stabilizer is reductive and does not
have the Borel-containing property defined in the same copy.
That older wording is not attributed to the changed-title
2020 publication. Its controlling published proof and the
measure classification and non-divergence chain remain
external gates. ASV Proposition~2.11's local tangent argument
also needs a noncritical parametrization; a nonconstant
holomorphic map need not have nonzero derivative at an
arbitrary center. Rouch\'e intersection requires a restricted
defining function that is not identically zero.

The earlier Picard-jump mechanism was read in actual~1--6
of the twelve-page \cite{Oguiso00PicardV1}, including the
Main Theorem and its local period/Rouch\'e proof. Its layout
is distinct from the combined 2003 JAG article and does
not itself give strong density for the new prescribed
lattice-divisor collection. In ASV's construction immediately
before Proposition~2.6, actual~8, mere integrality and
divisibility one are used to choose an isotropic pairing-one
partner. The odd unimodular Gram matrix
$\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\1&1\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$
shows why an evenness or additional application argument is
needed. The entropy preprint's Proposition~2.9 avoids that
demand by allowing the partner's square to be arbitrary.
Neither diagnostic supplies a counterexample to the new
density theorem or a certified replacement proof.

The frozen source audit contains eighteen scoped records
and six elementary diagnostics. Its actual reading totals
ninety-one physical pages in forty-six displayed sheets;
the integration adds no fresh primary-page audit. The
source's finite-index, positive-cone, discriminant-action
and local Hilbert-scheme inputs keep the detailed qualifications
recorded there. No canonical problem status, original result,
independent model call or production receipt is added.
In particular, \eqref{eq:hybrid-HK-dense-existence} supplies
neither the family-uniform estimate
\eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-uniformity-input} nor the mixed-product
input for \eqref{eq:hybrid-K3-conditional-limit}. It provides
no general BJ22 homogeneous Siu/envelope division assertion.

\section{Unbounded boundary obstacles and divisor Green approximation}
\label{sec:provenance-boundary-envelopes}

\Cref{sec:appH-boundary-envelopes,sec:ytd-boundary-fixed-obstacle}
use two exact first versions: Dao--Nguyen,
\cite{DaoNguyen26UnboundedDirichletV1}, arXiv:2610.00914v1,
submitted 1 October 2026, 01:46:02 UTC; and Dao--Nguyen--Tran,
\cite{DaoNguyenTran26HypersurfaceGreenV1}, arXiv:2610.00909v1,
submitted the same day, 01:39:03 UTC. Their complete returned
thirty-three- and thirty-five-page PDFs were read as full text and
displayed. These are attributed source conclusions, not independent
certifications of their complete external proof chains.

The Dirichlet paper's Theorem~1.1, actual~3 and proof~14--15,
requires extended continuity on the closed bounded domain and
ordinary real superharmonicity inside. It proves local boundedness
and maximality of the interior psh envelope, including extended
boundary limits at barrier points. The obstacle is not an arbitrary
continuous function, and its upper envelope is not interchangeable
with the Perron envelope of arbitrary boundary data. Definitions~1.2--1.3
and Theorem~1.4, actual~4--5 and proof~15--18, use maps on a fixed
closure neighborhood and real-analytic inverse trajectories. The
moving-boundary cluster uses the closed domain; the identity map
has the entire boundary as its cluster. The one-sided obstacle
condition has a finite target value, and the cluster avoids the
infinite locus with the stated barriers. No nesting or Runge
property of moving domains is assumed.

Theorem~1.4's increasing whole-boundary approximation differs from
Lemma~3.3 and Corollary~3.4's nonmonotone convergence off a
neighborhood pluripolar hull. Proposition~3.6, actual~19--20,
requires the stronger finite-truncation boundary control for
whole-domain convergence. Example~5.2, actual~28--29, prevents
replacement by bare pointwise boundary convergence. The hull
representation in Theorem~1.5, actual~5--6 and proof~21, has its
own pseudoconvex ambient domain, holomorphically convex closure
and neighborhood-barrier inputs. It concerns an \(\mathcal O(U)\)
hull. Corollary~1.8, actual~23--24, requires uniform radius
convergence on the specified closed ambient set and domain
convergence from both sides. These further statements are not
inferred merely from interior continuity.

Theorem~1.9, actual~6--7 and proof~24, keeps the same obstacle,
a global lower bound, complete neighborhood pluripolarity of
the exceptional boundary set and barriers elsewhere. Its
comparison class explains the Poisson example on actual~24--25.
The comparison mechanism quoted in Appendix H is the selected
locally bounded maximal case of
\cite[Lemma~2.6(i), actual~8--9]{DieuLongHieu25QuasiBRegularV1}.
The read edition of that source is arXiv:2509.12063v1;
only actual~1--3 and~6--9 of its twenty-three-page PDF were read.
Its quasi upper bounded approximants, upper boundary limits for
the minorant and lower boundary limits for the comparator are
retained. Its domain boundary-pluripolar witness differs from
a neighborhood pluripolar hull. Part~(ii) uses a harmonic
comparator and a different polar exceptional set. The corresponding
2026 JGA article, DOI \nolinkurl{10.1007/s12220-026-02412-1}, was identified
from publication metadata, but its publisher PDF request returned
an HTML error page. The selected proof and v1 diagnostics are
not attributed to that unread published edition.

For the Green-function paper, Definition~1.4, actual~4--5,
requires a jointly holomorphic map on a full parameter disk,
an inward image of the entire closed domain on a complex sector,
uniform displacement, quantitative depth near the boundary divisor
and nonidentically-zero escape at singular boundary divisor points.
The defining function is reduced on a neighborhood of the closure.
Theorem~1.7, actual~5 and proof~29--33, first proves canonical
truncation approximation without B-regularity. B-regularity
subsequently makes the bounded envelopes continuous Perron
solutions. The pointwise residual identity is asserted off the
divisor; its upper-regularized identity holds throughout the
domain. Continuity of the Green function at the divisor follows
from the residual's local upper bound. Residual continuity on
the whole divisor is not a further conclusion imported here.

The local proof uses a finite Weierstrass cover, moving centers
selected by Lemma~5.5's submean inequality, and relative estimates
in Lemmas~5.1--5.4. It does not presume the residual's continuity
or holomorphic variation of individual roots. The singular relative
constant is independent of truncation; regular losses on short
arcs vanish at the required rate. The target contours and Rouch\'e
margins precede the source cutoffs and root-distribution integer.
The final radial shrink carries a logarithmic error. Proposition~2.1's
convexifying maps extend across both closures; the Reinhardt and
Kohn--Nirenberg examples retain their separate geometric and
external B-regularity inputs. An absence-of-boundary-discs criterion
is not imported for arbitrary domains.

The classical divisor input was read in the published
\cite{LarussonSigurdsson99GreenPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1512/iumj.1999.48.1767}: actual~1 and~11--22, printed~1513
and~1523--1534, including the full displayed Section~3.
Proposition~3.2 gives psh divisorial subtraction, whereas
Theorem~3.3 retains its Stein ambient and subspace-extension
hypotheses. The original Theorem~3.9, printed~1532--1533,
has the missing comparison explicitly identified in the complete
\cite{LarussonSigurdsson01GreenErratum}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1512/iumj.2001.50.2279}. The returned erratum has one
actual page, printed~1705; citation metadata extending to~1706
does not create a second read page. It says the theorem remains
unproved and the other results are unaffected. It distinguishes
global upper boundedness of the residual from its local bound
near the divisor. This is a proof gap, not a supplied counterexample.
The acquired older math/9807164v1 was not substituted for the
published copy or counted as read.

The full twenty-page \cite{Nguyen05HypersurfaceGreenPublished},
DOI \nolinkurl{10.1007/BF02383618}, was read. Its Theorem~2.2, printed~184--185
and proof~189--192, and Corollary~2.9 retain homogeneous hyperplanes,
radial trace geometry, closure hull and good-barrier inputs. Its
whole-domain residual continuity and non-B-regular star-shaped
examples keep their own scope. Radial preservation of a singular
homogeneous boundary pole fails the new escape test. Neither
result's hypotheses are silently substituted for the other's.

The functional interface and root-cell argument were read in
actual~1--4 and~9--14, printed~171--174 and~179--184, of the
twenty-two-page returned published layout of
\cite{DrinovecForstneric12DiscPublished}, DOI \nolinkurl{10.4064/ap106-0-13}.
Theorem~1.1 retains the manifold or locally irreducible space scope.
The functional definitions distinguish point Lelong weights from
divisor intersection orders. Lemma~3.2 and Theorem~3.1 fix source
arcs and target points before the large root integer; the spray
construction preserves the selected target points. The new
additive divisor gluing is a distinct construction. The printed
root-phase slash formula is not reproduced; the standard spacing
is \(2\pi j/k\). The metadata of arXiv:1109.3947v3 was consulted,
but that preprint was not the read edition. General manifold
splitting and the remaining external functional chain are not
fully audited by the selected pages.

Other literal auxiliary defects remain narrowly scoped: the 2005
example \(-\log(|f|+1/j)\) is not C2 at a simple zero; its
coordinate map in Corollary~2.11 acts on the wrong coordinate;
and its peak base in Proposition~3.2 needs normalization for a
general maximum M. The quasi-B-regular v1's positive power
\((-\log|z|)^\alpha\), \(0<\alpha<1\), is not psh.
These local diagnostics do not refute either new main theorem
or certify complete replacement proofs.

The frozen source review records twenty-four scoped entries,
twelve elementary checks, and 119 actually read source/page pairs
in sixty-two displayed sheets across seven exact PDFs. Four
returned PDFs were read completely; three were read in the
specified selections. The integration reuses that evidence and
adds no fresh primary-page reading, original result, independent
model call or production receipt. Its unsquared logarithms
retain \(\ddc\log|f|=\tfrac12[\operatorname{div}(f)]\) in the
book's normalization. The open complex directions on the Green
paper's actual~33--34 do not add canonical Chapter~72 atoms.
Scalar divisorial subtraction, SID approximation and obstacle
comparison do not repair general non-Archimedean Siu division
or the BJ22 Theorem~6.21 envelope-closure boundary.

\section{Unbounded-domain Perron comparison and source qualifications}
\label{sec:provenance-unbounded-domain-comparison}

\Cref{sec:appH-unbounded-domain-comparison} distinguishes bounded
capped data on unbounded domains from the unbounded obstacle on a
bounded domain in \Cref{sec:appH-boundary-envelopes}. Its controlling
recent source is \cite{Pawlaschyk26UnboundedPerronV1}, exact
arXiv:2606.16584v1, submitted 15 June 2026, 11:30:28 UTC.
All thirteen actual PDF pages were read. Definition~2.3 and
Theorem~2.8, actual~2--3, use the Hunt--Murray eigenvalue-count
class, distinct from p-plane trace positivity. Definition~2.11
and Remark~2.12, actual~3--4, specify the dual comparison class;
the determinant-zero interpretation is restricted to smooth functions.
No general positive Bedford--Taylor measure is assigned to the
non-psh q-class.

Definition~3.1 and Proposition~3.4, actual~5--6, retain the finite cap
and distinguish continuous closure competitors from usc ones.
Definition~4.15, actual~9, requires a negative psh witness continuous
in the open domain and uniformly tending to \(-\infty\) at infinity.
An enlarged primary view corrected an initial reading of \(C(D)\)
as \(C(\overline D)\); the controlling definition has no closure bar.
Proposition~4.17's printed continuous closure extension is not assumed
by that definition. Its bounded maximum argument can instead use the
witness's nonpositive boundary upper limits.

Theorem~4.19(1)--(4) and Remark~4.20, actual~10--12, supply the
attributed bounded existence and capped-envelope identification with
\(0\leq r\leq q\leq n-r-1\), r-peaks, bounded nonnegative f and
the stated infinity witness. Additive shifts treat bounded real data.
The source constructs a decreasing bounded-domain sequence, controls
the finite boundary with an extension, and uses translated capped
competitors to prove interior continuity. Classical q-Dirichlet,
semiconvex approximation and local comparison remain imported inputs;
reading the displayed argument does not certify their complete chain.

Theorem~4.19(5) and Claim~5, actual~10 and~12, literally omit growth
bounds on the competing q-Bremermann function. The complete elementary
exterior-disk check is \eqref{eq:appH-unbounded-exterior-family}:
all hypotheses hold for n=1,r=q=0, yet every \(c\log|z|\) has zero
finite-boundary trace. The positive multiple violates the first use
of a bounded-above maximum principle; the negative multiple violates
the asserted outer comparison with \(\varepsilon\Psi\). Thus that
uncapped uniqueness clause is false as written. The bounded uniqueness
in the book is the direct consequence of the attributed addition and
infinity maximum principles, not an import of this defective clause.

Other literal recent-source claims are kept separate. Example~2.13,
actual~4, does not justify an arbitrary holomorphic pullback of a
maximal log-norm potential: \(F(z)=(1,z)\) in \(\mathbf C\) has
\(\log\|F\|=\tfrac12\log(1+|z|^2)\) with positive Levi form.
The polynomial example in~4.16, actual~9, needs positive degree;
a constant polynomial would satisfy its inequality on every domain.
Remark~4.21, actual~12, literally takes the relative q-hulls of
\(\overline{D\cap B_r(0)}\) inside \(\overline D\). Since every compact
lies in its displayed hull, their union already equals \(\overline D\),
contrary to the claimed proper inclusion. On the exterior disk the
uncapped zero-data envelope is infinite in D. An intended hull of
boundary subsets is not silently substituted from the unread older source.

The entire returned published \cite{NguyenHung08UnboundedPublished},
DOI \nolinkurl{10.5802/aif.2388}, was read: twenty-five actual pages,
including a publisher wrapper and printed~1383--1406.
Definition~2.2, printed~1386, uses bounded maximal solutions for
bounded boundary data; Definition~2.6, printed~1389, uses a finite
negative psh infinity witness without requiring continuity.
Theorem~4.4, printed~1398--1400, expressly restricts uniqueness
to bounded solutions and retains isotopy, moving-boundary peak/Jensen
and real-regularity hypotheses for its existence statement.

Its full Jensen chain is not certified. The arbitrary-cone
Theorem~3.1, printed~1390--1392, lacks the minorant/negative-constant
condition making every envelope finite. On a one-point space,
the cone \(\{0\}\) and obstacle \(-1\) give an empty supremum,
whereas the subprobability infimum is \(-1\). The compact-support
cone \(A_1\) does not contain negative constants. Proposition~3.4's
negative obstacle lies outside Proposition~3.3's displayed nonnegative
signature. The real \(2n\)-dimensional mollifier printed on~1398
has factor \(\delta^{-n}\), rather than mass-one \(\delta^{-2n}\);
the following fixed-point inequality also drops a negative witness
term without first scaling it. Theorem~2.1(c)'s missing negative
exhaustion condition, the continuity step in Lemma~3.2 and the
nonproper transfer's infinity control retain separate audit gates.
These auxiliary diagnoses do not certify a replacement proof or
disprove the scoped bounded nonnegative existence assertion.

The published \cite{Slodkowski84BremermannPublished} has twenty-five
returned pages including its wrapper; actual~1--5 and~16--22,
printed~303--306 and~317--323, were read. Theorem~5.1 and
Lemma~4.2 supply the q-index addition interface. Theorem~5.3 and
Lemma~5.4, printed~320, require bounded open domains.
Theorem~5.6, printed~321, omits the lower bound r\(\leq\)q in its
parameter range. On the \(\mathbf C^3\) shell \(1<|z|<2\),
a defining function \(\rho+\rho^2\), with
\(\rho=(|z|^2-1)(|z|^2-4)\), is strictly 2-psh near both boundaries.
The literal range admits q=0. Inner value one and outer value zero
cannot extend continuously as psh: restriction to
\((1,\zeta,0)\) gives a bounded subharmonic function on
\(0<|\zeta|<\sqrt3\), extending to one at the removable center and
zero on the outer circle, contrary to the maximum principle.
The full modern range is retained instead. The selected minimum
proof also prints a maximum in its auxiliary class; its unread
approximation foundations are not declared verified.

The frozen source review records fifty actual source/page pairs on
twenty-six displayed sheets, two repeated details, twenty scoped
records and fifteen elementary checks. The integration reuses that
reading; it adds no fresh primary-page audit, original result,
independent model call or production receipt. Neither bounded
q-comparison nor this local infinity witness supplies general
non-Archimedean Siu division or repairs the BJ22 Theorem~6.21
envelope-closure boundary. Canonical problem statuses are unchanged.

\section{Conifold contractions, mass bounds and metric completion}
\label{sec:provenance-conifold-contractions}

\Cref{sec:hybrid-conifold-contractions} imports
\cite{GuyettMcGowan26ConifoldV1}, exact arXiv:2610.00942v1,
submitted 1 October 2026, 02:19:41 UTC. All twenty-eight actual
PDF pages were read. Theorem~1.1 permits non-closed Hermitian
backgrounds and gives bounded normalized potentials, compact-away
smooth estimates, a positive scalar limit independent of the
Hermitian perturbation, and subsequential potential limits.
Corollary~1.1 adds the closed K\"ahler background: the scalar becomes
the cohomological volume, the potential descends to normal $Y$, and
bounded-potential uniqueness gives convergence of the full family.
Theorem~1.2 additionally requires dimension three, ordinary double
points and a perturbation K\"ahler near the exceptional curves.
The resulting Gromov--Hausdorff conclusion retains these hypotheses.

The smooth positive-parameter existence theorem of Tosatti--Weinkove,
Collins--Tosatti's closed nef/big null-locus theorem, EGZ bounded
singular K\"ahler uniqueness and the full regularity/descent chains
remain external inputs. The selected primary dependencies below
verify their stated interfaces; they do not certify every underlying
proof. No original theorem or independent verification receipt is
claimed by this integration.

The published \cite{GuedjLu22VolumesPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.14231/AG-2022-021}, has twenty-six actual pages,
printed~688--713. Actual~1--4 and~10--17 were read.
Proposition~3.2, printed~700, gives monotonicity of lower and upper
mass for ordered semipositive forms. Corollary~3.8, printed~704,
gives positive lower and finite upper Hermitian mass on Fujiki
manifolds. Lower positivity for the degenerate pullback in the new
source is separately obtained by comparison with a closed big form.
Positive background volume alone is insufficient for an arbitrary
non-closed semipositive form, as Example~3.5, printed~702, shows.
The literal proper-map statement in Lemma~3.6 is used here only for
degree-one modifications, where its mass change of variables applies.

\cite{GuedjLu21Envelopes3V1} is exact arXiv:2107.01938v1, submitted
5 July 2021, 11:12:56 UTC. Its thirty-four-page layout is distinct
from the unavailable 2023 Crelle edition. The cumulative reading
covers actual~1--13, 17--18 and~26--34: twenty-four pages, including
fifteen previously read pages and nine fresh ones. Theorem~A on
actual~2 requires positive lower mass, probability measure, a
normalized psh $L^m$ bound with $m>n$, and control of the ratio
of the equation constant to lower mass. Theorem~B separates
uniqueness of the untwisted scalar from uniqueness for the
positive-exponential equation. Theorem~4.2's local trace proof,
actual~26--30, is read with its singular barrier and torsion terms.
Its original unbounded-product warning is distinguished from the
later published product construction below. The v1 condition~(B)
has a bidegree typo in its upper bound; the correct squared-form
bound is retained from GL2, not copied from that literal line.

The published \cite{BoucksomGuedjLu25VolumesPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1007/s42543-025-00105-2}, appeared online on
16 June 2025. The returned PDF has forty-three pages; actual~1--5,
13--18 and~35--41 were read, eighteen pages. Theorem~2.2 extends
non-pluripolar products to quasi-closed, quasi-positive currents
under bounded mass. Theorem~4.7 and the introduction explicitly
distinguish a unique normalizing scalar from the largely open
untwisted potential uniqueness problem. The final continuation of
that proof on actual~42 and the full volume-continuity argument
are not declared read. Earlier arXiv pages are not relabeled as
published-edition reading.

\cite{Song12ConifoldV1} is the complete twenty-one-page exact
arXiv:1201.4358v1, submitted 20 January 2012, 18:22:06 UTC.
Its 2015 publisher PDF was unavailable and is not declared read.
Proposition~4.1, actual~10--13, is a leafwise trace calculation
using Ricci-flat K\"ahler symmetry; Proposition~4.2, actual~14--16,
combines a local flat embedding, determinant control and a
holomorphic radial vector. Its auxiliary vector has a transverse
\(-w\partial_w\) component and becomes the radial vector on
the selected \(w=0\) ruling after a unitary coordinate change.
The partial trace is not an inverse ambient form.
Propositions~4.3--4.4, actual~17--18, yield the exceptional-curve
and neighborhood diameter bounds. Their external diameter and
completion dependencies are not completely audited here.

Literal v1 presentation defects retain separate scopes. The
introductory class order differs from the controlling
\(\omega_t=\theta+t\omega_0\) and exceptional area formula;
the ansatz interval and rank-three line are incompatible with
their surrounding variables. The displayed ODE has an extra outer
derivative, while differentiating its cubic gives the intended
\((t+u')u'u''=e^{2\rho}\). The radial path's displayed endpoint
needs reciprocal radius rather than reciprocal squared radius.
These auxiliary issues do not give a main-theorem counterexample
or a conclusion about the unread journal edition.

The published \cite{Zhang25TangentConesPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1515/crelle-2025-0044}, has thirty-two actual pages,
printed~271--302. Actual~1--4 and~19--24 were read, ten pages.
Theorem~1.4, printed~273, requires an isolated klt algebraic germ,
agreement of the K-semistable and K-polystable Fano cones, a
smooth link, bounded potential and non-positive Ricci curvature.
The following cone-germ remark applies to the ordinary double
point. Its separate GH-limit Theorem~1.1 includes a quadratic
curvature bound; a unique smooth-link tangent cone alone does
not imply conical convergence. Section~7 uses earlier weighted
construction and external compactness/algebraic tangent-cone
results. Section~7.3's positive-Ricci program is not imported as
an established theorem.

The new source's Lemma~4.1 and Proposition~4.2, actual~18--21,
make the exceptional curve and source intrinsic diameter argument
explicit. Proposition~5.1, actual~23--26, separately requires
small target extrinsic diameter and source intrinsic diameter,
as well as smooth convergence on a regular core and the
topological identification of the completion. Figure~1's caption
reverses the displayed sides; the graph comparison itself supplies
the controlling direction. Corollary~1.1 on actual~2 prints an
X-volume in a Y-equation; actual~14 correctly uses the pushed
volume \(\Omega_Y\), as in \eqref{eq:hybrid-conifold-closed-limit}.

One initial reading error is explicitly withdrawn. The frozen
main sheet-six note called the first actual-page-11 torsion
estimate too strong. Redisplaying actual~9--12 shows absolute
values on both sides. With \(Q\geq0\), its form is
\(|B-2Q|\leq|B|+2Q\leq|B|+CR\); the following Young estimate
also bounds \(|B|\). The step is valid as printed. The initial
note remains archived with an image-controlled correction and
is not a source defect or proof repair.

The frozen review records 113 distinct exact-source/page pairs
on fifty-eight sheets: ninety-eight fresh pairs on fifty sheets
and fifteen reused GL3 pairs on eight sheets. Two repeated
main-paper sheets add no pages. Two PDFs are fully read and
four partially read. Twenty-four scoped records and twelve
elementary diagnostics are reused; the book integration adds
no fresh primary-page audit. No entire external proof chain,
new original result, independent model call, production receipt
or canonical Chapter~72 solution is certified. The fixed-resolution
parameter does not identify a hybrid skeletal metric limit, and
these complex inputs do not repair general non-Archimedean Siu
division or the BJ22 Theorem~6.21 envelope-closure boundary.

\section{Griffiths positivity and pointwise Chern forms}
\label{sec:provenance-Griffiths-Chern}

\Cref{sec:appH-Griffiths-Chern} imports
\cite{Du26GriffithsTopChernV1}, exact arXiv:2610.05931v1,
submitted 5 October 2026, 07:48:50 UTC. All fourteen actual PDF
pages were read. Theorem~1.1 gives, for every $r\geq9$, a smooth
Griffiths-positive metric on $\mathcal O_{\mathbf P^r}(1)^{\oplus r}$
whose top Chern form is negative on an open set. Theorem~2.3 computes
$F_9(\Phi_t)=-1536t^3$; Proposition~3.1 gives strict trace perturbation
and higher ranks. Proposition~3.2, actual~12--13, realizes a prescribed
strictly positive curvature map near a point by a global metric,
equal to the direct-sum Fubini--Study metric outside an arbitrary
prescribed neighborhood. These are statements about a smooth
metric-dependent representative, not a failure of numerical
ample-bundle positivity.

The published \cite{Finski22CharacteristicPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1112/jlms.12605}, has forty-one actual pages, printed
1539--1579. Actual~1--5, 7--12, 17--21 and~24 were read:
seventeen pages. Theorem~1.1 gives positive Schur forms under
Nakano or dual Nakano positivity. Theorem~1.3 identifies the
universal Griffiths Schur question with a positive-map
double-mixed-discriminant question. Proposition~1.4(c) is the
two-dimensional matrix case. Proposition~2.11's rank-one/spectral
proof, actual~11--12, supplies the curvature positivity dictionary.
The read proof of Theorem~1.3 on actual~20 imports the form-pushforward
Theorem~3.2; its full technical proof is not certified by this selection.
The equivalence of universal questions is not an equivalence for
every fixed rank's entire Schur family. The quadratic recipe of
Proposition~2.10 is used only through the directly normalized negative
jet in \eqref{eq:appH-Chern-local-volume}, fixing its sign and $2\pi$.

The published \cite{CaraccioloSokalSportiello13CayleyPublished}, DOI
\nolinkurl{10.1016/j.aam.2012.12.001}, has 121 actual pages,
printed~474--594. Actual~1--4 and~17--19 were read, seven pages.
Theorem~2.1, printed~477, uses independent entries and permits
constant translations of the matrix. Its elementary proof on
printed~490--492 counts permutation cycles after Jacobi's complement
minor identity. At size three and exponent two the factor is
$2\cdot3\cdot4=24$. No symmetric-matrix half-derivative convention
or claim to have read all 121 pages is substituted.

\cite{Hall06PositiveMapsV1} is exact quant-ph/0607035v1, submitted
5 July 2006, 13:02:59 UTC. Actual~1 and~8--10 were read in its
sixteen-page file. The printed 17 July 2018 typesetting date does
not replace that arXiv version date. Its title differs from the
journal title cited in the new source; the journal edition was
not read. Theorem~4 was checked in this v1, on actual~9, with
its preceding rank-one argument. It gives positivity of
$\operatorname{tr}(S)I-S-US^TU^*$ for unitary skew $U$.
Adjacent Lemma~3's general complex-skew normality assertion and
the repeated ket-bra in equation~(29) are not used: odd-dimensional
skew singularity follows from the determinant, and
\eqref{eq:appH-Chern-nine-map} uses the explicit unitary $J$.
These auxiliary issues do not refute the required projection argument.

\cite{Wan26ThirdChernV2} is exact arXiv:2601.10424v2,
submitted 20 January 2026, 08:44:14 UTC. All sixteen actual pages
were read. Theorem~0.3 and Theorem~2.11 give the three-dimensional
strictly-positive-map result; the all-Schur corollary retains
rank three and dimension three. The proof uses exact scaling,
normalized sphere moments and Schur's eigenvalue inequality.
Lemma~2.6's final moment formula has the correct denominator;
an extra prefactor in its intermediate projector line is not copied.
Theorem~0.5's arbitrary-rank $c_3$ interface is explained through
pointwise principal curvature blocks and tangent-plane restriction.
An arbitrary holomorphic subbundle with induced metric need not
retain Griffiths positivity. The external signed-Segre theorem of
G\"uler and semipositive inputs of Fagioli remain attributed rather
than certified by this sixteen-page reading.

\cite{Idel16MatrixScalingV1} is exact arXiv:1609.06349v1,
submitted 20 September 2016, 20:41:04 UTC. Its returned file
has 101 pages; actual~1, 4, 55--56, 59--60, 97--98 and~100--101
were read, ten pages. Theorem~1.2 gives exact invertible scaling
for positivity-improving maps. Mere rank non-decrease gives an
approximate-scaling problem with different hypotheses. The correct
capacity stationarity equation is
$H^*(H(C)^{-1})=C^{-1}$, without the extra outer inverse printed
in equation~(79). General indecomposable uniqueness calculations
and adjacent approximate-scaling wording are not imported.
The original complete Gurvits proof remains an external input;
this selected review is not relabeled as a reading of that original.

The frozen source review contains 68 distinct exact-source/page
pairs on thirty-five sheets: two full PDFs and four partial
selections. Twenty-four scoped records and twenty-one exact
elementary diagnostics are reused by this integration. They check
the bilinear Gaussian pairing, Cayley constants, determinant
factorization, coefficient $-1536$, metric cutoff errors,
mixed-discriminant normalization and principal-compression boundary.
The book edit adds no new primary-source reading, original theorem,
independent model call or production receipt. The least negative
top-Chern rank remains a source-scoped literature direction, without
a new canonical problem atom or an exhaustive intervening-literature
claim. No non-Archimedean higher Chern construction, singular
product or repair of the BJ22 Theorem~6.21 Siu/envelope boundary
is asserted.
